All Claude Code mods/Usage meters

Context and usage meters for Claude Code

892 mods that show how full your context window is, how much of your plan’s 5-hour and weekly windows you have used or the session’s estimated cost so far, or warn you before a limit. Each name opens the mod’s page with its hooks, API calls, access and installation guidance.

Browse A to ZABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ0-9Other
Browse by topicPrompt cacheUsage meters

What these mods do

Two different measures, in Anthropic’s words. The context window is what one session holds. When it fills, Claude Code compacts it, at a threshold that depends on the model and the configuration, and /context shows what fills it (Anthropic: when your context fills up). Account consumption is separate: a subscription has a 5-hour window and a weekly window, shared across Claude’s surfaces, plus model-specific windows where the plan has them, and /usage shows the ones the account reports (how usage and length limits work, Claude Code: when a developer asks about a limit). Cost is an estimate: Claude Code computes a session’s dollar figure locally from token counts and list or configured prices, on an API key as well as on a subscription, and the bill lives in the Console or at the provider (Claude Code: track your costs, API pricing).

A mod reads all three through $.session.usage, which returns the context fill, the account’s rate-limit windows and the estimated session cost, with readings the harness lacks left absent (Anthropic: reach files, processes and the network). Some mods fetch figures from elsewhere or compute their own. They draw them as a band above the prompt, a status line, a pane, a toast or a notification, as bars, rings, weather or a pet, and some warn or ask before a limit.

How to choose one

  • Check what it measures: this session, the whole account, or one part of it such as subagents or one tool. The description says which, and the README says how it reads the figure.
  • Read the calls line on the mod’s page, and the README, for the job each call does. A recorded $.session.usage reads the harness’s own figures. A recorded $.http.fetch can be a gateway’s figures coming in or figures going out. A recorded model method or $.prompt.submit is a request on your plan or API key, which may belong to a warmer, a compaction step or something else (Anthropic: call a model). A program the mod starts reaches the network with your own access, which network policy does not cover (Anthropic: which controls still apply). Of the 863 mods here with reported footprints, 788 record $.session.usage, 74 record $.http.fetch, 262 record a process call and 110 record a $.model method or $.prompt.submit.
  • A dollar figure is an estimate at API prices, and mods do not all use the same prices. A subscription’s allowance is not a bill.
  • Read the hooks line. A prompt.submit hook sees matching prompts, or every prompt when no filter is recorded (Anthropic: review what a mod can do). When the footprint is incomplete, event coverage is unknown. 218 of the 892 mods here record a prompt.submit hook, 23 of them with an incomplete footprint, where coverage is unknown.
  • The scan is static. It lists what the code references, not what it does at runtime. A level does not limit what a program the mod starts can do. “Reads mod state” means the scan saw a state read and does not record whose state. See what a mod can access.
